# arXiv query design for the Reliability, DOE and SPM tracks

Part 1 is the follow-up analysis of 2026-10-08 (term-by-term tests, eight reference journals, gpt-6-luna labels). Part 2 is the first-round report, kept for the record; where the two disagree, Part 1 is the current evidence. This document is generated by `10_build_report.py` from `report_followup.py`, `report_text.py` and tables in `cache/`. The choice of query for each track is the owner's.

# Part 1. Follow-up: term-by-term analysis on eight journals

## 1.1 Short version

**Revised queries, assembled only from terms that passed a per-term test.** Sources: `cache/v2/score_*.csv`. Gold = journal articles with an arXiv version, labelled on topic by Luna and screened by the analyst (86 reliability, 74 DOE, 56 SPM). "On topic" for new papers is the Luna v2 label on a seeded random sample of 40.

| track | candidate | papers (current query) | gold recall (current) | silver in scope kept | silver out of scope removed | new papers on topic, n = 40 | request line through httr (limit 4,094) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reliability | `R7_kept_terms_stat_only` | 655 (704) | 44 of 86 (29 of 86) | 269 of 316 | 349 of 381 | 29 of 40 | 3,174 |
| Reliability, broader | `R8_kept_terms_plus_clauses` | 2,332 (704) | 63 of 86 | 270 of 316 | 349 of 381 | 33 of 40 | 3,903 |
| DOE | `D5_kept_terms` | 2,022 (771) | 50 of 74 (10 of 74) | 674 of 678 | 0 of 96 | 32 of 40 | 1,482 |
| DOE, higher recall | `D7_kept_terms_plus_clauses` | 2,695 (771) | 68 of 74 | 676 of 678 | 0 of 96 | 25 of 40 | 2,468 |
| SPM | `S6_kept_terms` | 305 (157) | 44 of 56 (24 of 56) | 142 of 142 | 0 of 13 | 29 of 40, of which 8 also carry the change-detection-theory flag | 915 |
| SPM, theory excluded | `S7_kept_terms_strict` | 246 (157) | 37 of 56 | 142 of 142 | 0 of 13 | 26 of 40, none with the theory flag | 805 |

All queries are printed verbatim in section 1.8 and were run against the arXiv API.

**Main findings.**

1. *Bundles hide weak terms.* Tested one at a time under a stricter rubric, several terms of the first-round recommendations fail: for reliability `abs:"reliability analysis"` (11 of 25 sampled new papers on topic unrestricted, 14 of 25 inside the statistics categories), `abs:"degradation data"` (8 of 25), `ti:weibull` (4 of 25), `ti:"failure time"` (7 of 25); for DOE `ti:"computer experiments"` inside the statistics categories (12 of 25) and every abstract-level form of the current title phrases (`abs:"response surface"` 7 of 25, `abs:"experimental design"` 12 of 25 inside the statistics categories).
2. *Many terms do not match what they appear to.* The API stems words, drops a leading stopword and treats a stopword inside a phrase as a placeholder. `abs:"in-control"` is `abs:control` (238,603 against 238,814 results); `ti:"A-optimal"` is `ti:optimal` (79,414 against 79,446); `ti:"burn-in"` is `ti:burning` (692 each); `ti:repairable` is `ti:repair` (1,777 against 1,779); `ti:prognostics` is `ti:prognostic` (271 each); `abs:dependability` is `abs:depend` (402,817 each); `ti:"blocked design"` is `ti:"block design"`; `ti:"A/B testing"` is `ti:"B testing"` (114 each); `ti:"computer experiments"` is `ti:"computational experiments"` (129 each). `abs:"change-point"` does not match the one-word spelling "changepoint" (1,582 against 362). Section 1.4.
3. *The journals publish almost no sequential change-detection theory.* Of 13,780 articles in the eight journals I read 8 as quickest or sequential change-detection theory (ASMBI 4, QREI 3, CSDA 1; none in IISE or IIE Transactions, Technometrics, JQT, Quality Engineering or IEEE Transactions on Reliability), and none uses the vocabulary of e-detectors, e-processes, test martingales, conformal martingales or anytime-valid inference. They do publish sequential change detection *methods framed as monitoring* (20 found, 7 of them in Technometrics). Section 1.6.
4. *For SPM the scope decision moves the answer.* If theory-flagged papers count, `abs:"out-of-control"`, `abs:"control limits"`, `abs:EWMA` and `abs:Shewhart` (all inside the statistics categories) pass and the query reaches 44 of 56 gold articles. If they do not count, only the profile-monitoring terms pass and recall is 37 of 56. `abs:"average run length"`, `abs:"run length"`, `abs:CUSUM`, `abs:"change-point detection"` and the quickest-detection phrases fail either way as single terms.
5. *The R client imposes a hard length limit.* aRxiv 0.18 sends the query in a GET request line. The arXiv server rejects a request line over 4,094 bytes ("Request Line is too large (4402 > 4094)", HTTP 400), and httr percent-encodes more characters than a browser does. A first version of R8 (3,069 characters) failed for that reason; the assembly rule was tightened and the present R8 (2,649 characters, request line 3,903) is delivered, with about 190 bytes of headroom. Section 1.9.

**What rests on what.**

- gpt-6-luna with the v2 rubric: the topic labels of all 13,780 journal articles; the on-topic shares in every per-term, per-clause and per-candidate table (9,604 arXiv papers labelled).
- The analyst's reading: screening of the gold candidates (250 titles, 34 vetoed), the classification of change-detection articles in section 1.6, and two blind agreement checks (80 journal articles, 120 arXiv papers).
- gpt-5.2 silver flags (frozen corpus): "in scope kept", "out of scope removed" and the clause tests in section 1.5.
- Keyword rubric: nothing in Part 1 except its comparison with Luna in section 1.3.

## 1.2 Eight-journal reference corpus

OpenAlex sources were resolved by ISSN and the returned journal name asserted (`01_fetch_openalex.py`). IIE Transactions is the predecessor title of IISE Transactions and is a separate OpenAlex source (S63835026); its 2010 to 2016 articles are included. arXiv versions come from OpenAlex locations plus a Semantic Scholar lookup by DOI, checked by title similarity against arXiv metadata (1,077 candidate pairs, 1,068 accepted; `cache/journal_arxiv_matches.csv`). Where OpenAlex has no abstract the Semantic Scholar abstract is used, then the abstract of the matched arXiv version (`cache/journal_text.csv`). Topic counts are Luna v2 labels; an article can carry several. Source: `cache/journal_coverage_v2.csv`.

Notes.

- *Computational Statistics & Data Analysis.* OpenAlex has abstracts for only 10% of its 3,275 research items (Elsevier). Semantic Scholar raises that to 42%; the other 1,894 articles were labelled from the title and OpenAlex keywords and topics. Luna labelled 69 reliability, 126 DOE and 23 SPM articles; only those enter the vocabulary and gold sets, and only 3, 12 and 1 of them have an arXiv version. Topic counts for this journal are probably undercounts.
- *IEEE Transactions on Reliability* has 77 DOE-labelled articles (1 with an arXiv version) and 23 SPM-labelled articles (none with an arXiv version). They are reported here and kept out of the DOE and SPM gold sets and vocabulary.
- *IISE Transactions* runs focus issues, including Quality and Reliability Engineering; OpenAlex does not record the focus issue, so the topic labels are the only split available (IISE and IIE together: 280 reliability, 77 DOE, 128 SPM).

**Expanded gold sets** (`18_build_gold_v2.py`, `hand_labels/gold_v2_review.json`, `hand_labels/gold_ids_v2.json`). Candidates are articles with an accepted arXiv version that Luna labelled on topic, plus the first-round hand gold. I screened every candidate by title, and by abstract where the title was not enough: reliability 101 candidates, 15 vetoed, 86 gold; DOE 93, 19 vetoed, 74 gold; SPM 56, none vetoed, 56 gold. For SPM the expansion adds 12 articles to the first-round 44: ASMBI 6, IISE Transactions 4, CSDA 1, and one Technometrics article accepted on Luna's label.

## 1.3 Rubric v2 and agreement

The v2 rubric (`00_rubric_v2.R`) defines each topic by what the reference journals publish: a label is TRUE only when the main contribution is framed around an engineered product, process, quality characteristic, engineered system or experiment. It excludes "reliable" machine learning, generic lifetime-distribution and survival work, clinical-trial designs, bandit theory, surrogate modelling without a design component, offline change-point analysis and process mining, and it carries a fourth label, CHANGE_THEORY, for sequential or quickest change-detection theory.

Agreement tables (`19_handcheck_v2.py`, `cache/v2/agreement_v2.md`). The hand samples were drawn stratified on the Luna label and shown to me shuffled and without the Luna labels.

| scope | label A | label B | topic | n | agree | A yes, B yes | A yes, B no | A no, B yes | A no, B no |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| all journal articles with both labels | keyword rubric | Luna v2 | reliability | 13780 | 12747 (93%) | 3432 | 691 | 342 | 9315 |
| all journal articles with both labels | keyword rubric | Luna v2 | doe | 13780 | 13285 (96%) | 880 | 182 | 313 | 12405 |
| all journal articles with both labels | keyword rubric | Luna v2 | spm | 13780 | 13347 (97%) | 1814 | 345 | 88 | 11533 |
| journal sample, 80 articles, eight journals (blind) | Luna v2 | analyst hand label | reliability | 80 | 79 (99%) | 26 | 1 | 0 | 53 |
| journal sample (articles that have a keyword label) | keyword rubric | analyst hand label | reliability | 80 | 76 (95%) | 26 | 4 | 0 | 50 |
| journal sample, 80 articles, eight journals (blind) | Luna v2 | analyst hand label | doe | 80 | 77 (96%) | 19 | 3 | 0 | 58 |
| journal sample (articles that have a keyword label) | keyword rubric | analyst hand label | doe | 80 | 73 (91%) | 12 | 0 | 7 | 61 |
| journal sample, 80 articles, eight journals (blind) | Luna v2 | analyst hand label | spm | 80 | 80 (100%) | 21 | 0 | 0 | 59 |
| journal sample (articles that have a keyword label) | keyword rubric | analyst hand label | spm | 80 | 78 (98%) | 20 | 1 | 1 | 58 |
| arXiv papers from the term samples, reliability (blind) | Luna v2 | analyst hand label | reliability | 40 | 32 (80%) | 15 | 5 | 3 | 17 |
| arXiv papers from the term samples, exp_design (blind) | Luna v2 | analyst hand label | doe | 40 | 32 (80%) | 12 | 8 | 0 | 20 |
| arXiv papers from the term samples, spc (blind) | Luna v2 | analyst hand label | spm | 40 | 27 (68%) | 7 | 13 | 0 | 20 |
| arXiv papers from the term samples, spc (blind) | Luna v2, SPM and not change-theory flag | analyst hand label | spm | 40 | 34 (85%) | 6 | 5 | 1 | 28 |

Reading.

- On journal articles Luna v2 and my blind labels agree on 79, 77 and 80 of 80 articles. The keyword rubric and Luna agree on 93 to 97% of all 13,780 articles; the keyword rubric over-labels reliability (691 articles it marks that Luna does not) and misses DOE (313 the other way).
- On arXiv papers Luna is more inclusive than my reading. Of the 20 papers per track that Luna labelled on topic I agreed with 15 (reliability), 12 (DOE) and 7 (SPM). Luna almost never rejected a paper I accepted (3, 0 and 0 cases). The on-topic shares in the tables below are therefore upper estimates; the ranking of terms is more trustworthy than the level.
- For SPM, 8 of the 13 disagreements are quickest or sequential change-detection theory papers to which Luna gave both SPM = TRUE and CHANGE_THEORY = TRUE. Not counting theory-flagged papers raises agreement from 27 to 34 of 40. That flag is not clean either: among sampled papers carrying both labels there are genuine chart papers (for example "Signed Sequential Rank CUSUMs", a gold article, and "Optimal Design of the Shiryaev-Roberts Chart"). The SPM tables therefore report both counts, and the truth lies between them.
- The app's own screen prompt was not used for these labels and was not edited.

## 1.4 Tokenisation and stemming traps

Every candidate term was checked two ways: the share of its matches that contain the term literally (column "literal share" in the per-term tables; a low value means the API matched something else), and explicit count probes (`17_token_probes.py`, `cache/token_probes.csv`). Equal counts mean the API does not distinguish the two spellings.

Terms that do not match what they appear to, and what to do about each:

| term as written | what the API matches | evidence | consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `abs:"in-control"` | `abs:control` | 238,603 and 238,814 results | unusable |
| `ti:"A-optimal"` | `ti:optimal` | 79,414 and 79,446 | unusable; `"D-optimal"` is safe (82), `"I-optimal"` is not (literal share 0.26, matches "I. Optimization") |
| `ti:"burn-in"` | `ti:burn`, `ti:burning` | 692 each; 681 of 692 titles are non-literal | unusable |
| `ti:repairable` | `ti:repair` | 1,777 and 1,779 | use the phrase `"repairable system"` (16) |
| `ti:prognostics` | `ti:prognostic` | 271 each | use `"prognostics and health management"` |
| `ti:censored` | `ti:censor`, `ti:censoring` | 832 and 833; includes "cosmic censor" | dropped |
| `abs:dependability` | `abs:depend`, `abs:dependence` | 402,817 each | unusable |
| `ti:reliability` and every phrase built on it | `ti:reliable ...` | "reliability analysis" and "reliable analysis" 134 each; "reliability estimation" literal share 0.06 in abstracts | needs the context requirement or a title-only phrase |
| `ti:degradation` | `ti:degrade`, `degraded`, `degrading` | 1,707 each | "degradation model" matches "degraded models" (literal share 0.32 in abstracts) |
| `ti:"accelerated test"` | "acceleration test", "accelerating tests" | 30 each, literal share 0.17 | keep only inside the statistics categories |
| `ti:"design of experiment"` | any stopword in the middle: "design and experiments", "design for experiments" | 173 each | source of the "Design and Experiments" robotics titles |
| `ti:"experimental design"` family | "experiment" and "experience" share a stem | `ti:experiment` and `ti:experience` 18,908 each; "design experiment" and "design experience" 29 each | source of user-experience design titles |
| `ti:"computer experiments"` | "computational experiments", "computing experience" | 129 each, literal share 0.58 (0.16 in abstracts) | dropped as a single term; kept in conjunctions |
| `ti:"blocked design"` | `ti:"block design"` | identical sets (79) | one of the two is enough |
| `ti:"A/B testing"`, `"A/B tests"` | `"B testing"`, `"B tests"` | 114 each; matches "B-tests" | kept, restricted where needed |
| `ti:"order-of-addition"` in abstracts | "order ... addition" with any stopword | abstract literal share 0.05 | title form only, or inside the statistics categories |
| `ti:"optimal design"` | "optimally designed", "optimization design" | 612 each | kept only inside the statistics categories |
| `ti:"choice experiments"` | "delayed choice experiment" (physics) | literal share 0.31 | kept only inside the statistics categories |
| `abs:"change-point"` | not the spelling "changepoint" | 1,582 against 362 | both spellings are needed where the term is used |
| `ti:"multi-fidelity"` | not "multifidelity" | 415 against 116 | same |
| `abs:"Phase II monitoring"` | 2 results; `abs:"Phase monitoring"` gives 22 | roman numerals are kept as tokens | harmless, adds nothing |
| `abs:"out-of-control"` | "out of control" (191); "out control" gives 41 | the stopword position must be filled | safe |
| `abs:EWMA`, `abs:CUSUM` | case-insensitive (81 and 380) | | safe |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query` | answers HTTP 301 to the https address | `cache/url_length_probe.csv` | httr follows the redirect; each request costs two round trips |

**SPM (a): the 'process monitoring' clause**

Most of these papers are outside the frozen corpus, so the silver flags say little; the on-topic shares are in the per-term table.

| clause | papers | of which in frozen corpus, in scope | in frozen corpus, out of scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| "process monitoring" in title or abstract, unrestricted | 371 | 19 | 0 |
| removed by the process-mining exclusion (S_BPM) | 120 | 0 | 0 |
| kept when restricted to stat.ME or stat.AP | 52 | 15 | 0 |

Reading.

- *Reliability.* The whole precision problem sits in one title word: `ti:reliability` matches 207 in-scope and 351 out-of-scope papers (silver precision 0.37), while `ti:degradation` is at 0.75, `ti:maintenance` at 0.88 and `ti:"remaining useful life"` at 1.0. Requiring a context term removes 328 of those 351 at a cost of 40 in-scope papers. The context list is dominated by `abs:failure` (134 in scope admitted). It is also the leakiest term: 15 of the 32 out-of-scope papers that survive the requirement come in through `abs:failure` alone, against 24 in-scope papers that only it admits. Deleting it is a defensible stricter option. Nine of the 34 context terms admit no paper that another term does not already admit and could be deleted to shorten the query. The stat.ML clause is no longer a problem once context is required: stat.ML-only papers go from 72 in scope and 189 out of scope to 57 and 17.
- *DOE.* `ti:"experimental design"` carries the track (488 in scope, silver precision 0.94). `ti:"designed experiment"` is the weakest phrase (17 in scope, 12 out of scope) because it stems to "design(ing) experiment(s)". No exclusion is cheap except `ANDNOT cat:physics.ed-ph` (0 in scope lost, 4 out of scope removed). `ANDNOT cat:cs.HC` loses 8 to remove 15; the first-round D3 exclusion loses 15 to remove 26; no abstract term tested removes more than 9 out-of-scope papers. The 96 out-of-scope papers are spread thinly over many categories, so the practical way to deal with them is the app's relevance flag, not the query.
- *SPM.* The process-mining exclusion removes 120 of the 371 "process monitoring" papers and none of the frozen in-scope ones, but restricting the phrase to stat.ME or stat.AP is simpler and keeps 15 of the 19 in-scope papers it matches.

## 1.6 Do the eight journals publish sequential or quickest change-detection theory?

Method (`24_change_theory_table.py`, `hand_labels/change_theory_review.csv`). I searched titles and abstracts of all 13,780 articles for the wording, took every article that Luna flagged CHANGE_THEORY (104), read the titles and abstract openings of both sets, and classified the relevant ones into three kinds. The Luna flag alone is not usable as a count: it also fires on ordinary CUSUM, GLR and SPRT chart papers.

Facts for the owner.

- *Theory in the strict sense* (optimality, minimax or asymptotic results for stopping rules, or performance analysis posed as the quickest-detection problem, not framed around a process or quality characteristic): 8 articles in 17 years. ASMBI has 4 (three by the same group on the Shiryaev-Roberts and CUSUM procedures, one on a "disorder problem"), QREI 3 (two on performance criteria for sequential change detection, one relating two CUSUM formulations), CSDA 1 ("Optimal sequential detection by sparsity likelihood", 2024, the only one that states asymptotic lower bounds on detection delay). IISE and IIE Transactions, Technometrics, JQT, Quality Engineering and IEEE Transactions on Reliability have none.
- *E-detectors, e-processes, test martingales, conformal test martingales, anytime-valid inference, confidence sequences*: no article in any of the eight journals uses these terms in its title or abstract.
- *Sequential or online change detection methods framed as monitoring* (data streams, networks, sensors; evaluated by run length or detection delay): 20 found. Technometrics 7 (six of them published in 2022), QREI 5, IISE Transactions 3, ASMBI 2, CSDA 2, JQT 1. This list comes from the wording search and is not exhaustive.
- *Sequential tests for acceptance or reliability demonstration* (SPRT plans, start-up demonstration tests) are a separate, older line: 14 found, mostly IEEE Transactions on Reliability, QREI and Quality Engineering. They are not change detection.
- Caveat: 58% of CSDA articles have no abstract in the corpus, so for that journal the search is mostly on titles.

On arXiv the proportions are reversed. Among sampled new papers matched by `abs:"sequential change-point detection"` inside the statistics categories, Luna gave the SPM label to 8 of 25 and all 8 also carry the theory flag; for `abs:CUSUM` it is 5 of 25, all 5 theory-flagged; for `abs:"average run length"` 11 of 25, 7 theory-flagged (`cache/terms/spc_terms.csv`, columns `sample_on_topic` and `sample_on_topic_other_rule` of the strict run).

## 1.7 Per-term tables

Method (`13_term_fetch.py`, `14_term_tables.py`, generalised from the coordinator's `11_spm_term_marginals.py`). For each track a names-based base query is fixed. Every term is run through the API in the title and in the abstract. Four variants are reported: title, title inside the statistics categories, abstract, abstract inside the statistics categories. For each: papers matched; papers new beyond the base; gold articles recovered that the base misses; the on-topic share of a seeded random sample of up to 25 new papers (Luna v2); the dominant primary categories of the new papers; the literal share.

Verdict rule, applied mechanically. Keep a variant when at least 60% of its sample is on topic (with fewer than 8 sampled papers: at least 60% and either a gold article recovered or every sampled paper on topic) and the variant would add no more than 50 off-topic papers (new papers times one minus the on-topic share). An unrestricted variant that alone adds more than 1,000 papers is not sampled and is dropped as too broad. For an unrestricted variant the share is a stratified estimate: papers inside the statistics categories are judged from its own sample pooled with the restricted variant's sample, papers outside from its own sample. Assembly: for each term the broadest kept form is taken; forms are then added in order of gold recovered and on-topic share, and a form enters the query only if at that point it still adds at least 10 papers or one gold article.

Two thresholds were changed after seeing results, and I disclose them: the off-topic cap was first 40 and was raised to 50 so that `ti:"optimal design"` inside the statistics categories (218 new papers, 20 of 25 on topic, 9 gold articles) would not be dropped on volume alone; the minimum contribution was first 3 papers and was raised to 10 to bring the reliability query under the server's request-line limit.

### Reliability

Base: the current title words, with an engineering-context term required, inside stat.ME, stat.AP or stat.ML (the first-round R3). The terms tested are the phrases of the first-round R4, the remaining journal vocabulary, and further vocabulary from the journal tables.

Base query (309 papers, gold 27 of 86): `((ti:reliability OR ti:degradation OR ti:maintenance OR ti:"remaining useful life" OR ti:"failure analysis") AND (abs:failure OR ti:failure OR abs:lifetime OR abs:maintenance OR abs:"remaining useful life" OR abs:"reliability analysis" OR abs:"system reliability" OR abs:"structural reliability" OR abs:"reliability engineering" OR abs:"reliability assessment" OR abs:"reliability growth" OR abs:"reliability-based design" OR abs:weibull OR abs:prognostics OR abs:repairable OR abs:"life test" OR abs:hazard OR abs:"stress-strength" OR abs:"rare event" OR abs:"coherent system" OR abs:"series system" OR abs:"degradation model" OR abs:"degradation data" OR abs:"degradation process" OR abs:"degradation test" OR ti:"reliability analysis" OR ti:"system reliability" OR ti:"structural reliability" OR ti:"reliability assessment" OR ti:"reliability engineering" OR ti:"degradation model" OR ti:"degradation data" OR ti:"degradation test" OR ti:"stress-strength") AND (cat:stat.ME OR cat:stat.AP OR cat:stat.ML))`

Category restriction for the `+cat` variants: stat.ME, stat.AP, stat.ML, stat.CO, stat.OT. Sample size per variant: up to 25 new papers.

### SPM

Base: the coordinator's names-based base ("control chart", the SPC and SPM names, and "process monitoring" inside stat.ME or stat.AP). The first table counts a paper on topic when Luna gives the SPM label; the column `other rule` in `cache/terms/spc_terms.csv` and the second table do not count papers that also carry the change-detection-theory flag.

Base query (237 papers, gold 33 of 56): `((ti:"control chart" OR abs:"control chart") OR (ti:"statistical process monitoring" OR abs:"statistical process monitoring" OR ti:"statistical process control" OR abs:"statistical process control") OR ((ti:"process monitoring" OR abs:"process monitoring") AND (cat:stat.ME OR cat:stat.AP)))`

Category restriction for the `+cat` variants: stat.ME, stat.AP, stat.CO, stat.OT, stat.ML, math.ST. Sample size per variant: up to 25 new papers.

Comparison with the coordinator's per-term results. The two screens agree on what to drop: `"average run length"`, `"run length"`, CUSUM, `"false alarm rate"`, Hotelling and `"in-control"`. They differ on `"monitoring scheme"` (10 of 25 here against 20 of 30 with the app's screen), `"statistical monitoring"` and `"self-starting"` (kept by the sample rule here but they add fewer than 10 papers, so they are left out of the assembled query, as is `abs:"monitoring statistic"`), and this analysis adds `ti:"monitoring method"`, `ti:"profile monitoring"` and `ti:"time between events"`. The app's screen counts change-detection theory as in scope; the v2 rubric tries not to, and Luna only partly complies (section 1.3).

## 1.8 Revised queries and scoring

`20_score_v2.py`, `qd_candidates_v2.py`. Candidates per track: the current query; the first-round recommendation; the query assembled from the kept terms; a variant (all kept terms inside the statistics categories for reliability and DOE, the strict assembly for SPM); the kept-term query plus the clauses of section 1.7 that recover missed gold; and for SPM the coordinator's revised query verbatim. "New versus current" counts retrieved papers that are in neither the current result set nor the frozen corpus. The on-topic share is the Luna v2 label on a seeded random sample of 40 of those new papers; each candidate has its own sample, so differences of ten points between nested candidates are within sampling error. For SPM the next column gives how many of the on-topic papers also carry the theory flag. The last column is the length of the HTTP request line as the httr client builds it; the arXiv server's limit is 4,094.

### reliability

## 1.9 Can the R client send these queries?

aRxiv 0.18 (the version in `renv.lock`) calls `httr::GET("http://export.arxiv.org/api/query", query = list(search_query = ..., ...))`, so the query travels in the URL. Three checks were made.

1. Padded probes with Python (`21_url_length_probe.py`): a URL of 4,028 characters is answered normally; 4,508 characters and above return HTTP 400; 12,008 and above return HTTP 414.
2. Every candidate sent through httr itself, the way aRxiv does (`23_httr_get_check.R`, `cache/httr_get_check.csv`). All candidates in the table above return HTTP 200 with the expected totalResults. The earlier, longer version of R8 (3,069 query characters) returned HTTP 400 with the message "Request Line is too large (4402 > 4094)".
3. The same query is longer through httr than through Python or a browser, because httr percent-encodes spaces as `%20` and also encodes quotes, colons and parentheses: R8 is 2,649 characters as text, 3,330 in a Python URL and 3,903 in the httr request line.

Consequences. The limit is 4,094 bytes for the whole request line, which leaves roughly 2,750 characters of query text. R8 fits with about 190 bytes to spare; aRxiv adds `sortBy` and `sortOrder` parameters when asked to sort, which fit within that margin, but any further term added to R8 will not. R7, D5, D7, S6 and S7 are well inside. aRxiv could not be run here (it is not installed in this R library), so its exact request was reproduced with httr and not called. The http endpoint now redirects to https; httr follows the redirect.

## 1.10 Recommendations, gold still missed, and risks

### Reliability

`R7_kept_terms_stat_only` is the conservative choice: the first-round base (context required) plus the kept terms, all inside five statistics categories. 655 papers, 44 of 86 gold articles (current query: 29), 269 of 316 silver in-scope papers kept, 349 of 381 out-of-scope removed, 29 of 40 sampled new papers on topic.

`R8_kept_terms_plus_clauses` is the choice if the track should follow what IEEE Transactions on Reliability, QREI and IISE Transactions publish, which is largely outside the statistics categories: 2,332 papers, 63 of 86 gold, 33 of 40 sampled new papers on topic. It triples the corpus (2,021 papers to extract), sits close to the request-line limit, and its unrestricted title terms (`ti:"predictive maintenance"`, `abs:"useful life"`, `ti:"reliability analysis"`, `ti:"fault tree"`) bring in the cs.LG and eess.SY prognostics literature.

Gold still missed by R8 (23 of 86; listed in section 1.8). Thirteen are IEEE Transactions on Reliability articles, most of them from computer dependability and hardware reliability (cs.AR, cs.PF, cs.SE, cs.DC, cs.LO: storage systems, soft errors, fault injection, performability). Their wording is "dependability", "availability", "soft errors", "fault vulnerability". `abs:dependability` is unusable (it stems to "depend"); `(abs:"soft errors" AND abs:reliability)` was kept and recovers one. The rest are statistics papers whose text never uses a reliability phrase: recurrent-event models, Birnbaum-Saunders tests, image-based prognostics, accumulated damage, structure functions.

Risks specific to reliability. Both revised queries drop about 394 papers of the current corpus, 46 or 47 of them silver in scope. The 82% and 72% on-topic shares are Luna's; by my reading of the blind sample Luna accepts about one reliability paper in four that I would not.

### DOE

`D5_kept_terms`: the current query plus 13 unrestricted and 17 category-restricted term forms. 2,022 papers, 50 of 74 gold (current: 10), nothing lost from the current corpus, 32 of 40 sampled new papers on topic.

`D7_kept_terms_plus_clauses` adds conjunctions such as `(ti:"active learning" AND abs:surrogate)`, `(ti:"sequential design" AND abs:simulation)` and `(abs:"computer experiments" AND ti:design)`: 2,695 papers, 68 of 74 gold, 25 of 40 on topic in its sample.

Gold still missed by D7 (6 of 74): "Sequential Adaptive Design for Jump Regression Estimation" and "Profile Bayesian Optimization for Expensive Computer Experiments" (IISE Transactions), "Entropy-based adaptive design for contour finding and estimating reliability" (JQT), a utility-emulation paper (Technometrics), an e-commerce inventory-testing paper (ASMBI) and a constant-stress accelerated-life-test planning paper (QREI). Their wording is "adaptive design" and "Bayesian optimization", both tested and dropped as single terms (7 of 25 and, inside the statistics categories, 15 of 25 with about 270 off-topic additions).

Risks specific to DOE. Luna is the most inclusive here: of 20 arXiv papers it labelled DOE in the blind check I agreed with 12 (it accepted causal-inference and ANOVA papers and a Bayesian-optimisation paper). Read with that discount, the new-paper precision of D5 is nearer 50% than 80%. The 96 out-of-scope papers of the current corpus all remain. D5 is 2.6 times the current corpus (1,251 papers to extract).

### SPM

`S6_kept_terms` if sequential change detection papers that Luna labels SPM are acceptable in the track: 305 papers, 44 of 56 gold (current 24, the coordinator's revised query 41), 29 of 40 sampled new papers on topic of which 8 also carry the theory flag. It differs from the coordinator's revised query by dropping `"monitoring scheme"`, `"statistical monitoring"`, `"self-starting"` and `"Phase II monitoring"` and adding `ti:"monitoring method"`, `ti:"profile monitoring"` and `ti:"time between events"`.

`S7_kept_terms_strict` if they are not: 246 papers, 37 of 56 gold, 26 of 40 on topic, none theory-flagged. It is the base plus profile-monitoring terms only.

`S8_kept_terms_plus_clauses` reaches 48 of 56 gold with 342 papers, but only 21 of 40 of its sampled new papers were on topic; I do not recommend it on this evidence.

Gold still missed by S6 (12 of 56) and their wording. Six are online or sequential change detection or monitoring for high-dimensional or streaming data, five in Technometrics and one in JQT ("online monitoring", "data streams", "change-point detection", "real-time monitoring", "concept drift monitoring", "sequential change detection"; categories stat.ML, cs.LG, stat.ME). Two are monitoring papers listed only in cs.LG or eess.SY ("subspace monitoring", "cross-covariance monitoring"). One is a change-detection hypothesis test for a surface-finishing process (IISE Transactions). One is deep-learning fault detection (IISE Transactions). One has "control limits" in the title only (ASMBI). One is a quickest-detection comparison of CUSUM and Shiryaev-Roberts (the ASMBI article counted as theory in section 1.6). As single terms the wording fails (`abs:"change-point detection"` 2 of 25 inside the statistics categories, `abs:"online monitoring"` 8 of 25, `abs:"data streams"` 2 of 25, `abs:"real-time monitoring"` 2 of 25). As conjunctions some pass on small samples, for example `(ti:monitoring AND abs:"false alarm")` inside the statistics categories (9 of 13, of which 4 without the theory flag) and `(abs:"average run length" AND abs:chart)`; they are what S8 adds.

### Risks common to all three

- *Luna shares are relative.* Samples are 25 per term variant and 40 per candidate; Luna is more inclusive than my reading on arXiv papers (section 1.3). Use the tables to rank terms, not to promise a precision.
- *The verdict thresholds are mine* (60% on topic, at most 50 off-topic additions, at least 10 papers or one gold article). The per-term tables give the counts, so the owner can apply a different bar term by term.
- *Gold sets are skewed.* 1,068 of 13,780 articles have an arXiv version; Technometrics and CSDA authors post far more often than QREI, IEEE Transactions on Reliability or Quality Engineering authors.
- *Assembled queries are unions of many small terms.* Each passed alone; their joint precision was measured only through the 40-paper samples.
- *Stemming is undocumented behaviour* and can change. `17_token_probes.py` reruns the probes.
- *arXiv throttling.* During this work the API answered HTTP 429 in bursts; `qd_arxiv.py` now waits 4 seconds between calls and backs off for 45 seconds or more after a 429.
- *Corpus growth.* The revised queries add 148 (S6) to 2,021 (R8) papers that would each need a factsheet extraction.

## 1.11 What changed from the first round, what was not done, and cost

- The first-round recommendations (R4, D2, S3) are superseded as recommendations; they are scored again in section 1.8 against the expanded gold. The first-round hand labels used a looser reading (theory counted as SPM, clinical-trial designs counted as DOE) and are not used in Part 1.
- `08_label_new_samples.R` (Luna v1 on the first-round samples) was not run; it is superseded by `15_label_arxiv_samples.R`.
- Not done: hand labels for the 40-paper candidate samples (only the 120-paper blind check was read by me); a test through the aRxiv package itself; any edit to the app's prompts or scope texts.
- Cost of the follow-up, from `cache/token_usage.csv` at the prices in the app's `R/usage.R` (USD 0.10 per 1M input tokens, USD 0.50 per 1M output tokens): 13,780 journal articles, 15.9M input and 1.56M output tokens, USD 2.37; 9,604 arXiv papers, 11.3M input and 1.27M output tokens, USD 1.77. Total USD 4.14 against a cap of USD 8. No HTTP 429 with exhausted credit occurred. OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar and arXiv calls were free.

# Part 2. First-round report (2026-10-08, four journals, keyword stand-in and analyst hand labels)

The text and tables below are the first-round report. Its tables are the files written in the first round (`cache/score_tables.md`, `cache/vocab_tables.md`, `cache/handcheck_agreement.md`); steps 04, 05, 07 and 09 now read the eight-journal corpus, so rerunning them changes those tables and the numbers quoted in this part.

## arXiv query design for the Reliability, DOE and SPM tracks

Analysis date: 2026-10-08. Frozen corpus: `data/frozen/v1/`. All numbers below come from files written by the numbered scripts in this directory (file names are given next to each table). This document is generated by `10_build_report.py` from the prose in `report_text.py`.

This report presents options and evidence. The choice of query for each track is the owner's.

### 1. Short version

**Recommended starting points (one per track), with the main trade-off.**

| track | recommended candidate | papers retrieved (current query) | silver in-scope papers kept | silver out-of-scope papers removed | journal-gold recall (current query) | on-topic share of newly retrieved papers, hand sample of 40 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reliability | `R4_context_plus_specific_phrases` | 472 (704) | 268 of 316 (85%) | 349 of 381 (92%) | 29 of 42 (23 of 42) | 33 of 40 (82%, 95% CI 68 to 91%) |
| DOE | `D2_add_stat_title_vocab` | 1,377 (771) | 674 of 678 (99%) | 0 of 96 | 30 of 52 (9 of 52) | 32 of 40 (80%, 95% CI 65 to 90%) |
| SPM | `S3_names_plus_stat_monitoring_terms` | 340 (157) | 142 of 142 | 0 of 13 | 32 of 44 (23 of 44) | 31 of 40 (78%, 95% CI 62 to 88%) |

The queries are printed verbatim in section 6. Sources: `cache/score_reliability.csv`, `cache/score_exp_design.csv`, `cache/score_spc.csv`.

**Three findings that drive the recommendations.**

1. The arXiv API stems search terms. `ti:reliability` and `ti:reliable` return the identical set (205 results each inside stat.ME, `cache/arxiv_probes.csv`). 219 of the 376 out-of-scope reliability papers have the word "reliable" in the title, against 14 of 316 in-scope papers and 2 of 1,718 journal reliability titles (`cache/phrase_coverage_reliability.csv`). The noise in the reliability track is therefore mostly this one stem, and it cannot be removed with `ANDNOT ti:reliable` because that would also remove "reliability". Requiring an engineering term in the abstract is what separates the two senses.
2. The current DOE query matches the arXiv vocabulary ("experimental design" is in 429 of 674 in-scope arXiv titles) but not the journal vocabulary ("experimental design" is in 24 of 798 journal DOE titles; "split plot", "computer experiments", "factorial", "optimal design(s)" and "screening" are common in the journals and nearly absent from the current arXiv corpus). Only 9 of 52 journal DOE articles that have an arXiv version are retrieved by the current query.
3. For SPM, adding the phrase "process monitoring" without restriction is expensive. Of 40 hand-checked new papers it brings in, 11 were on topic. The phrase is also the name of a process-mining task ("predictive business process monitoring") and a generic engineering term. Adding the field names "statistical process control" and "statistical process monitoring" is cheap (55 new papers, 30 of 40 hand-checked on topic), and "process monitoring" becomes usable when restricted to stat.ME or stat.AP.

**What rests on what.**

- Verified by reading (analyst hand labels): the journal-gold sets (291 matched articles screened by arXiv title, unclear ones by abstract), the on-topic share of newly retrieved papers (612 arXiv papers labelled from the title plus the opening of the abstract), and the 80-article check of the journal topic labels.
- Rests on the existing gpt-5.2 silver flags: every "in scope kept" and "out of scope removed" count.
- Rests on the keyword stand-in rubric: the journal topic labels used for the vocabulary tables, and the column "keyword stand-in (all new)" in the scoring tables.
- Rests on gpt-6-luna: nothing. Only 12 journal articles were labelled before the API account ran out of credit (section 2).

### 2. What could not be done

- **Luna labelling.** The plan was to label all 5,614 journal articles and up to 150 newly retrieved papers per candidate with `gpt-6-luna`. The first test batch of 12 articles succeeded (`cache/journal_labels.csv`, `cache/token_usage.csv`: 11,872 input and 1,299 output tokens). After that the coordinator reported that the OpenAI account had no credit left (HTTP 429), so no further calls were made. The scripts are in place and resumable: `02_label_journal_articles.R` and `08_label_new_samples.R`. Once labels exist, `05_vocabulary.py luna`, `07_score_candidates.py` and `09_handcheck_agreement.py` pick them up.
- **Stand-in used instead.** Journal topic labels come from a transparent keyword rubric (`qd_rubric.py`). The precision of newly retrieved papers comes from hand labels by the analyst, with larger samples than originally asked (40 per candidate instead of 30, 612 papers in total).
- **Who the "hand" is.** The hand labels were made by the analysis agent (Claude), not by the owner or another human. For arXiv papers the agent read the title and the first 150 to 250 characters of the abstract, not the whole abstract and not the PDF. Labels were not blind to which candidate a paper came from.
- **The R package aRxiv is not installed**, so queries were run with a small Python client against `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query` (`qd_arxiv.py`). The app uses aRxiv; the recommended queries are long (up to 1,488 characters) and were not tested through that package.
- **Cost.** One labelling call of 12 items: 11,872 input tokens (about $0.001 at $0.10 per 1M) and 1,299 output tokens (the output price was not given to me). Projected input cost of the full Luna run at the observed 990 input tokens per item: about 6,770 items (5,602 journal articles plus 1,168 sampled arXiv papers), 6.7M tokens, about $0.67, plus roughly 0.7M output tokens. OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar and arXiv calls were free.

### 3. Data coverage

**Journal reference corpus (OpenAlex, publication years 2010 to 2026).** Source ids were resolved from the ISSN and the returned journal name was asserted to match. File: `cache/journal_coverage.csv`.

| journal | ISSN | OpenAlex source | works 2010 to 2026 | kept as research items | with abstract (100+ characters) | with keywords or topics | arXiv id in OpenAlex |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Journal of Quality Technology | 0022-4065 | S166831694 | 702 | 600 | 548 | 593 | 15 |
| Technometrics | 0040-1706 | S985303 | 1,275 | 996 | 913 | 977 | 76 |
| Quality Engineering | 0898-2112 | S13624874 | 1,369 | 1,075 | 901 | 1,073 | 5 |
| Quality and Reliability Engineering International | 0748-8017 | S165633816 | 3,216 | 2,943 | 2,912 | 2,936 | 13 |
| all four | | | 6,562 | 5,614 | 5,274 (94%) | 5,579 | 109 |

"Kept" means OpenAlex type article or review and a title that does not start with an editorial marker (erratum, book review, editorial, and so on; the pattern is in `01_fetch_openalex.py`). The filter is imperfect. Some book reviews whose title is the book title remain; they fall in the "other" class. Abstract coverage is lowest for Quality Engineering (84%) and Journal of Quality Technology (91%); articles without an abstract are labelled from the title and keywords only.

**arXiv versions of journal articles (the gold pool).** OpenAlex alone links only 109 of the 5,614 articles to arXiv, so a second query-independent source was added: the Semantic Scholar batch endpoint, looked up by DOI (5,257 articles have a DOI). Together they give 294 candidate pairs (99 found by both, 185 by Semantic Scholar only, 10 by OpenAlex only). Each arXiv id was fetched from the arXiv API and the two titles compared; 291 pairs with a normalised title similarity of at least 0.60 were accepted (Technometrics 198, Journal of Quality Technology 41, Quality and Reliability Engineering International 37, Quality Engineering 15). The 3 rejected pairs look like genuine retitled papers, so the threshold is conservative. File: `cache/journal_arxiv_matches.csv`. No title search against arXiv was used, so the pool does not depend on any candidate query.

**Frozen arXiv corpus.** The factsheet files hold several versions of some papers. Everything here is counted at paper level (arXiv id without version, flag of the highest version). That gives 709 reliability papers (316 in scope, 381 out of scope, 12 without a flag), 775 DOE papers (678, 96, 1) and 157 SPM papers (142, 13, 2). These differ from the row counts quoted in the task (416 of 756, 99 of 837, 16 of 165) only because of the version duplicates. Five out-of-scope reliability papers and four in-scope DOE papers are in the factsheet but not in the metadata file and are not returned by the current query today; they appear as "removed" or "lost" for every candidate, including the current one.

### 4. Labelling rubric and how far to trust the labels

**Rubric.** The same three definitions are used everywhere. The full text sent to Luna is the constant `RUBRIC` in `00_common.R`. In short:

- Reliability: failure-time, lifetime and degradation modelling of engineered items, remaining useful life and prognostics, maintenance and inspection policies, warranty, accelerated tests and test plans, reliability growth, system, structural or software reliability, repairable systems, failure mode analysis, stress-strength, lifetime distributions framed for life testing. Not reliability of a measurement or rater, not reliability or calibration of machine-learning predictions, not "reliable" as an adjective, not degradation of images or model performance, not biomedical survival analysis without an engineering framing.
- DOE: construction, evaluation or analysis of designed experiments (factorial, screening, split-plot, mixture, optimal, space-filling, computer experiments, sequential or Bayesian design, online experiments), response surface methodology, robust parameter design, or applied studies whose central method is a designed experiment. Not papers that merely run experiments, not apparatus or lab setups, not "response surface" as a physical surface.
- SPM: control charts of any kind, Phase I and Phase II analysis, run-length properties, sequential change detection or surveillance framed as monitoring, process capability, monitoring-oriented fault detection in industrial processes. Not feedback control, not offline change-point estimation, not generic outlier detection.

**Keyword stand-in (`qd_rubric.py`).** A topic is assigned when the title or the keywords contain at least one term from that topic's list, or the abstract contains at least two distinct terms, unless a title-level exclusion matches. It labels 1,718 journal articles reliability, 798 DOE, 1,757 SPM and 1,643 none of the three (`cache/journal_labelled_kw.csv`; multiple labels allowed).

**Hand check of the journal labels.** A seeded random sample of 80 articles (20 per rubric-positive topic and 20 rubric-negative) was read (title and opening of the abstract). All three labels were judged for each article. Files: `hand_labels/journal_handcheck.csv`, `cache/handcheck_agreement.md`.

| scope | automatic label | topic | n | agreement | auto yes, hand yes | auto yes, hand no | auto no, hand yes | auto no, hand no |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| journal sample (80) | keyword rubric | reliability | 80 | 77/80 (96%) | 21 | 3 | 0 | 56 |
| journal sample, rubric-positive stratum only (20) | keyword rubric | reliability | 20 | 19/20 (95%) | 19 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| journal articles with a Luna label | keyword rubric vs gpt-6-luna (Luna in the 'hand' columns) | reliability | 12 | 12/12 (100%) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 12 |
| journal sample (80) | keyword rubric | doe | 80 | 74/80 (92%) | 18 | 4 | 2 | 56 |
| journal sample, rubric-positive stratum only (20) | keyword rubric | doe | 20 | 17/20 (85%) | 17 | 3 | 0 | 0 |
| journal articles with a Luna label | keyword rubric vs gpt-6-luna (Luna in the 'hand' columns) | doe | 12 | 11/12 (92%) | 1 | 1 | 0 | 10 |
| journal sample (80) | keyword rubric | spm | 80 | 78/80 (98%) | 24 | 1 | 1 | 54 |
| journal sample, rubric-positive stratum only (20) | keyword rubric | spm | 20 | 19/20 (95%) | 19 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| journal articles with a Luna label | keyword rubric vs gpt-6-luna (Luna in the 'hand' columns) | spm | 12 | 12/12 (100%) | 9 | 0 | 0 | 3 |
| new arXiv papers, reliability | keyword rubric | reliability | 102 | 81/102 (79%) | 59 | 14 | 7 | 22 |
| new arXiv papers, exp_design | keyword rubric | doe | 145 | 124/145 (86%) | 102 | 14 | 7 | 22 |
| new arXiv papers, spc | keyword rubric | spm | 212 | 157/212 (74%) | 88 | 28 | 27 | 69 |

Reading: on journal articles the keyword rubric agreed with the hand label in 77, 74 and 78 of 80 cases for reliability, DOE and SPM. Its errors were over-inclusion (for example "Optimal Design of a CUSUM Chart" labelled DOE, a special-issue editorial labelled SPM) and a few misses (two DOE papers and one process-capability paper in the negative stratum). The sample is stratified, so these are not population accuracy figures. On arXiv papers the same rubric is much weaker (74 to 86% agreement with the hand labels, with errors in both directions), which is why the scoring tables report it only as a secondary column. The single Luna batch (12 articles) agreed with the keyword rubric on 35 of 36 labels; that is too small to say anything about Luna.

**Judgement calls in the hand labels** that a different reader might make differently:

- Reliability: papers proposing lifetime distributions (Weibull extensions and similar) were counted on topic when the abstract framed them for failure or life-test data, off topic when the framing was biomedical or generic. Rare-event and failure-probability simulation papers were counted on topic when framed as reliability analysis of engineering systems.
- DOE: clinical-trial designs (dose finding, stepped wedge) were counted on topic when the contribution was a design; A/B testing and online experiments were counted on topic; pure Gaussian-process emulation without a design component was excluded from the gold set but counted on topic in the new-paper samples when the paper was framed as a computer experiment.
- SPM: sequential and quickest change detection theory was counted on topic; offline change-point tests were not; neural-network fault detection for industrial processes was counted on topic; predictive business process monitoring was not.

### 5. How the arXiv API matches terms

Probes in `cache/arxiv_probes.csv` (run by `06_run_candidates.py`):

| query | results |
|---|---|
| `ti:reliability AND cat:stat.ME` | 205 |
| `ti:reliable AND cat:stat.ME` | 205 |
| `ti:"reliability analysis" AND cat:stat.ME` | 16 |
| `ti:"reliable analysis" AND cat:stat.ME` | 16 |
| `ti:"design of experiment"` | 173 |
| `ti:"design and experiments"` | 173 |
| `ti:"experimental design" AND cat:cs.HC` | 12 |
| `ti:"experience design" AND cat:cs.HC` | 9 |
| `(ti:"control chart" OR abs:"control chart")` | 157 |

Consequences. Terms are stemmed, so "reliability" also matches "reliable", "repairable" matches "repair", "prognostics" matches "prognostic" and "experiment" overlaps with "experience" (several out-of-scope DOE papers are user-experience design papers in cs.HC). Stopwords and punctuation inside a quoted phrase are ignored, so `ti:"design of experiment"` also returns robotics and physics papers titled "... Design and Experiments". Single ambiguous words therefore need either a second condition or a category restriction; multi-word technical phrases are much safer.

### 6. Candidate queries (verbatim)

All queries were run against `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query` on 2026-10-08, paged with `max_results=1000` and a pause of at least 3.2 seconds between calls. For every candidate the number of parsed records equals the `totalResults` reported by the API (`cache/candidate_sizes.csv`). The current queries return 704, 771 and 157 records, the same as the frozen metadata files.

Building blocks (defined in `qd_candidates.py`):

- `R_TI` = `(ti:reliability OR ti:degradation OR ti:maintenance OR ti:"remaining useful life" OR ti:"failure analysis")`
- `R_ENG` = `(abs:failure OR ti:failure OR abs:lifetime OR abs:maintenance OR abs:"remaining useful life" OR abs:"reliability analysis" OR abs:"system reliability" OR abs:"structural reliability" OR abs:"reliability engineering" OR abs:"reliability assessment" OR abs:"reliability growth" OR abs:"reliability-based design" OR abs:weibull OR abs:prognostics OR abs:repairable OR abs:"life test" OR abs:hazard OR abs:"stress-strength" OR abs:"rare event" OR abs:"coherent system" OR abs:"series system" OR abs:"degradation model" OR abs:"degradation data" OR abs:"degradation process" OR abs:"degradation test" OR ti:"reliability analysis" OR ti:"system reliability" OR ti:"structural reliability" OR ti:"reliability assessment" OR ti:"reliability engineering" OR ti:"degradation model" OR ti:"degradation data" OR ti:"degradation test" OR ti:"stress-strength")`
- `R_TP` = `(ti:weibull OR ti:"life test" OR ti:"accelerated life" OR ti:"accelerated degradation" OR ti:warranty OR ti:"repairable system" OR ti:"failure time" OR ti:"failure data" OR ti:"step-stress" OR ti:"stress-strength" OR ti:"lifetime data" OR ti:"lifetime distribution" OR ti:"lifetime prediction" OR ti:"prognostics and health management")`
- `R_AP` = `(abs:"reliability analysis" OR abs:"system reliability" OR abs:"structural reliability" OR abs:"software reliability" OR abs:"reliability engineering" OR abs:"remaining useful life" OR abs:"accelerated life" OR abs:"accelerated degradation" OR abs:"predictive maintenance" OR abs:"condition-based maintenance" OR abs:"repairable system" OR abs:"lifetime data" OR abs:"degradation data" OR abs:"life test" OR abs:"stress-strength")`
- `R_SPECIFIC` = `(abs:"life test" OR abs:"accelerated life" OR abs:"accelerated degradation" OR ti:"step-stress" OR abs:"remaining useful life" OR abs:"degradation data" OR ti:"failure data" OR ti:"lifetime prediction" OR abs:"repairable system" OR abs:"stress-strength" OR ti:warranty OR abs:"condition-based maintenance" OR abs:"reliability analysis" OR abs:"software reliability" OR abs:"reliability growth")`
- `D_TP` = `(ti:"factorial design" OR ti:"fractional factorial" OR ti:"split-plot" OR ti:"space-filling design" OR ti:"latin hypercube" OR ti:"screening design" OR ti:"screening experiment" OR ti:"definitive screening" OR ti:"robust parameter design" OR ti:"mixture experiment" OR ti:"order-of-addition")`
- `D_TA` = `(ti:"optimal design" OR ti:"computer experiments" OR ti:"orthogonal array" OR ti:"space-filling" OR ti:"online experiments" OR ti:"controlled experiments" OR ti:"A/B testing")`
- `D_TB` = `(ti:"sequential design" OR ti:"adaptive design" OR ti:"Bayesian design")`
- `D_AP` = `(abs:"experimental design" OR abs:"design of experiments" OR abs:"designed experiment" OR abs:"optimal design" OR abs:"response surface" OR abs:"computer experiments" OR abs:"factorial design" OR abs:"space-filling")`
- `S_NAMES` = `(ti:"statistical process monitoring" OR abs:"statistical process monitoring" OR ti:"statistical process control" OR abs:"statistical process control")`
- `S_PM` = `(ti:"process monitoring" OR abs:"process monitoring")`
- `S_TOOLS` = `(abs:"average run length" OR abs:"profile monitoring" OR abs:"control limits" OR abs:EWMA OR abs:"Phase II monitoring")`
- `S_CPD` = `(abs:"sequential change-point detection" OR abs:"online change-point detection" OR abs:"sequential change detection" OR abs:"quickest change detection" OR abs:"quickest detection")`
- `S_BPM` = `(abs:"business process" OR abs:"process mining" OR abs:"event log" OR ti:"predictive process monitoring" OR ti:"prescriptive process monitoring")`

**S3_names_plus_stat_monitoring_terms** (340 results reported by the API, 340 parsed, 509 characters)

```
((ti:"control chart" OR abs:"control chart") OR (ti:"statistical process monitoring" OR abs:"statistical process monitoring" OR ti:"statistical process control" OR abs:"statistical process control") OR ((ti:"process monitoring" OR abs:"process monitoring") AND (cat:stat.ME OR cat:stat.AP)) OR ((abs:"average run length" OR abs:"profile monitoring" OR abs:"control limits" OR abs:EWMA OR abs:"Phase II monitoring") AND (cat:stat.ME OR cat:stat.AP OR cat:stat.CO OR cat:stat.OT OR cat:stat.ML OR cat:math.ST)))
```

**S4_plus_sequential_change_detection** (472 results reported by the API, 472 parsed, 695 characters)

```
((ti:"control chart" OR abs:"control chart") OR (ti:"statistical process monitoring" OR abs:"statistical process monitoring" OR ti:"statistical process control" OR abs:"statistical process control") OR ((ti:"process monitoring" OR abs:"process monitoring") AND (cat:stat.ME OR cat:stat.AP)) OR (((abs:"average run length" OR abs:"profile monitoring" OR abs:"control limits" OR abs:EWMA OR abs:"Phase II monitoring") OR (abs:"sequential change-point detection" OR abs:"online change-point detection" OR abs:"sequential change detection" OR abs:"quickest change detection" OR abs:"quickest detection")) AND (cat:stat.ME OR cat:stat.AP OR cat:stat.CO OR cat:stat.OT OR cat:stat.ML OR cat:math.ST)))
```


How the candidates were arrived at. A first set was written from the vocabulary tables in the appendix. After the first round of hand labels three things were changed and the affected candidates re-sampled: (a) for reliability the journal-vocabulary block was narrowed to the phrases that were specific in the sample (`R_SPECIFIC`), because single words such as `ti:lifetime`, `ti:repairable`, `ti:prognostics` and the phrase `ti:"failure time"` pulled in customer lifetime value, "repair" in machine learning, biomedical prognostic models and accelerated failure time survival models; (b) for DOE `ti:"active learning"` was dropped (519 mostly machine-learning papers) and `ti:"orthogonal array"` and `ti:"computer experiments"` were moved inside the statistics categories; (c) for SPM the unrestricted `abs:CUSUM` was dropped (offline CUSUM change-point tests) and "process monitoring" was restricted to stat.ME or stat.AP. Because R4, S3 and S4 were tuned this way, their hand samples are partly but not fully independent of the tuning: 34 of 40 (R4), 23 of 40 (S3) and 31 of 40 (S4) sampled papers were labelled after the query was fixed. A last change added the title forms of nine context phrases to `R_ENG`; it changed only which frozen-corpus papers R2 to R5 keep (five more in scope, three more out of scope for R3 and R4), not the newly retrieved sets.

### 7. Scoring

Columns. "Retrieved" is the size of the result set. "Silver in scope kept" and "silver out of scope removed" compare with the frozen corpus and rest on the gpt-5.2 flags. "Gold recall" is the share of hand-labelled journal articles with an arXiv version that the query retrieves; it is independent of the current query and of the silver flags. "New papers" are retrieved papers not in the frozen corpus. "New on-topic, hand sample" is the analyst's label on a seeded random sample of 40 new papers per candidate, with a 95% Wilson interval. "Keyword stand-in" applies `qd_rubric.py` to all new papers and is shown for completeness; where it disagrees with the hand sample, trust the hand sample. "Est. overall precision" combines silver precision on the old part with the hand estimate on the new part, and "est. relevant papers" is the corresponding count; both inherit the sampling error of the hand estimate.

Gold sets (`hand_labels/gold_ids.json`, `cache/gold_*.csv`): 42 reliability articles (QREI 17, Technometrics 11, JQT 8, Quality Engineering 6), 52 DOE articles (Technometrics 29, JQT 14, QREI 6, Quality Engineering 3) and 44 SPM articles (Technometrics 16, JQT 14, QREI 10, Quality Engineering 4).

Each track section below has the scoring table, then for every candidate a random 15 of the in-scope papers it would lose and the first 15 (in seeded random order) of the papers it would gain, then the gold articles the current query misses. Full lists are in `cache/lost_*.csv` and `cache/gained_*.csv`. Paper titles are reproduced as they appear on arXiv.

### 8. Recommendation and risks

#### Reliability: `R4_context_plus_specific_phrases`

Reason. It is the only candidate that improves both sides. Against the current query it removes 349 of 381 silver out-of-scope papers, keeps 268 of 316 silver in-scope papers, raises journal-gold recall from 23 to 29 of 42, and the 163 papers it adds were on topic in 33 of 40 hand-checked cases. Estimated overall precision moves from 0.46 to about 0.87, with an estimated 402 relevant papers against 316 today.

Alternatives. `R3_context_required` is the same query without the added phrases: the smallest change that fixes precision (309 papers, silver precision 0.89), but it retrieves no new papers and gold recall stays at 22 of 42. `R2_statML_needs_context` keeps 301 of 316 in-scope papers and is the choice if losing existing in-scope papers is unacceptable, at a silver precision of only 0.60, because "reliable" titles in stat.ME and stat.AP stay. `R5_broad` reaches 33 of 42 gold articles but only 17 of 40 sampled new papers were on topic.

Risks.

- 48 silver in-scope papers are lost (a random 15 are listed in section 7). Reading that list, a part of them are doubtful silver positives (machine-learning "reliability" papers and comment or rejoinder notes), but genuine losses remain, for example degradation and acceptance-sampling papers whose abstracts use none of the context terms. The context list `R_ENG` is the lever: adding terms recovers recall and costs precision.
- The "in scope" definition that produced the silver flags and my hand labels differ at the margins (lifetime distributions, rare-event simulation). The 82% figure would be lower under a stricter reading.
- 13 of 42 gold articles are still missed. Four are outside the five statistics categories (eess.SY, cs.SE, cs.DC). The others are statistics papers on recurrent events, time-to-failure, lifetime data or prognostics whose title and abstract do not use the query's terms.
- The categories remain stat.ME, stat.AP, stat.ML, stat.CO and stat.OT. Reliability work posted only in eess.SY or cs.LG is not retrieved by design. `R5_broad` shows the cost of opening that door with the present vocabulary.

#### DOE: `D2_add_stat_title_vocab`

Reason. It keeps the whole current result set and adds the journal vocabulary. Gold recall rises from 9 to 30 of 52 and 32 of 40 sampled new papers were on topic. The result set grows from 771 to 1,377.

Alternatives. `D1_add_title_vocab` is the conservative step (166 new papers, gold recall 17 of 52). `D3_D2_minus_noisy_cats` additionally drops cs.HC, cs.RO and physics.ed-ph, removing 26 of 96 silver out-of-scope papers at the cost of 15 in-scope ones; its higher hand estimate (37 of 40) comes from an almost identical set of new papers (603 against 606), so the difference from D2 is sampling noise, not an effect of the exclusion. `D4_add_stat_abstracts` reaches 44 of 52 gold articles but adds 1,792 papers of which 24 of 40 sampled were on topic, many of them causal-inference and clinical-trial papers that mention an experimental design in passing.

Risks.

- Scope drift. D2 brings in optimal-design theory from math.ST, A/B testing and online experiments, and some clinical-trial design papers. I counted these on topic. If the owner wants an industrial-statistics DOE track only, the on-topic share of new papers is lower than 80%, and the phrases `ti:"A/B testing"`, `ti:"online experiments"` and `ti:"controlled experiments"` are the ones to drop.
- The 96 current out-of-scope papers all remain (12% of the corpus). They come from stemming and stopword behaviour on the existing title phrases ("Design and Experiments", "user experience design", "response surfaces" in physics). D3 is the only candidate that addresses this, bluntly.
- 606 new papers would need factsheet extraction.
- 22 of 52 gold articles are still missed, mostly sequential-design and active-learning papers for simulation experiments whose titles name Gaussian processes, surrogates or calibration and no design phrase from the query. D4 reaches most of them only by matching abstracts.

#### SPM: `S3_names_plus_stat_monitoring_terms`

Reason. It answers the renaming question with the best balance found. It keeps all 157 current papers, adds the SPC and SPM field names anywhere, adds "process monitoring" only inside stat.ME or stat.AP, and adds a short list of monitoring terms inside statistics categories. Gold recall rises from 23 to 32 of 44 and 31 of 40 sampled new papers were on topic.

On the specific question asked. Adding `"statistical process control"` and `"statistical process monitoring"` (`S1`) captures 6 more gold articles (29 of 44) for 55 new papers, 30 of 40 sampled on topic. The off-topic ones use "statistical process control" as a borrowed idea in another field or mention it in passing. Adding unrestricted `"process monitoring"` (`S2`) adds one more gold article and 335 further papers; 11 of 40 sampled new papers were on topic, and estimated overall precision falls from 0.92 to about 0.46. Excluding process-mining vocabulary (`S2x`) removes 120 papers but the sampled share stays near 30% (12 of 40), because most remaining matches are engineering papers that use "process monitoring" for sensing or machine-learning work without a statistical monitoring method.

Alternative. `S4_plus_sequential_change_detection` adds sequential and quickest change detection inside statistics categories (472 papers, gold recall 33 of 44, 34 of 40 sampled on topic). Whether that theory-heavy literature (math.ST, often cross-listed in cs.IT) belongs in a track called Statistical Process Monitoring is a scope decision. I labelled it on topic, which is why S4 scores well.

Risks.

- The 78% estimate has a wide interval (62 to 88%) and 17 of the 40 sampled papers were labelled before the query was fixed.
- 12 of 44 gold articles are still missed, mainly monitoring and online change-point papers posted in stat.ML or cs.LG that never use chart vocabulary.
- `abs:EWMA` and `abs:"control limits"` are restricted to statistics categories because both terms occur in finance and control engineering.

#### Risks common to all three

- **Small and uneven gold sets.** 42, 52 and 44 articles, with Technometrics and JQT over-represented because their authors post to arXiv more often than QREI and Quality Engineering authors. Gold recall describes the statistics-heavy end of each topic.
- **Sample size.** Each hand estimate is 40 papers. Differences of less than about 15 percentage points between candidates should not be read as real.
- **Single reader, shallow read.** Hand labels come from one AI reader using titles and abstract openings. Agreement with an independent labeller (Luna or a human) has not been measured.
- **Silver flags are not ground truth.** Some papers flagged in scope by gpt-5.2 look out of scope on title and abstract (several appear in the R3 and R4 "lost" lists), and the reverse will also occur.
- **Stemming is undocumented behaviour.** If arXiv changes how the API analyses terms, result sets will change. The probe queries in `06_run_candidates.py` can be rerun to detect that.
- **Operational cost.** Switching queries adds papers that need gpt-5.2 factsheet extraction (about 163 for R4, 606 for D2, 183 for S3) and, for reliability, raises the question of whether the 395 current papers that R4 no longer retrieves (48 in scope, 344 out of scope, 3 unflagged) should be removed from the displayed corpus or kept with their flags. Neither was costed here.
- **Query length.** R4 is 1,488 characters, D2 is 695 and S3 is 509. They run through a plain GET request; the R package aRxiv was not available to test.

### 9. How to rerun

See `README.md`. In order: `01` (OpenAlex), `03` (arXiv matching), `04` (keyword labels and hand-check sample), `05` (vocabulary), `06` (run candidates), `07` (score), `09` (agreement), `10` (this report). Steps `02` and `08` are the Luna labelling steps to run once the API account has credit.

### Appendix A. Distinguishing vocabulary and arXiv categories

Method (`05_vocabulary.py`). For each track three groups are compared: J, journal articles labelled on topic by the keyword rubric; I, arXiv papers in the frozen corpus flagged in scope; O, arXiv papers flagged out of scope. The statistic is the log-odds ratio of document frequency, (J plus I) against O, with a 0.5 pseudo-count, divided by its standard error (z). Terms are one to three words. Each table shows the 20 highest-ranked single words and the 20 highest-ranked phrases on one side. Document counts are given for all three groups so the reader can see which group drives a term.

Caveats. J was selected by a keyword rubric, so the rubric's own terms rank high on the in-scope side by construction; the I column (silver flags, independent of the rubric) is the check. Publisher boilerplate ("John Wiley Sons Ltd") appears in journal abstracts and should be ignored. For SPM the O group has only 13 papers, so the out-of-scope side is not informative; the phrase-coverage table is the useful one there.

The exact-phrase coverage tables answer a different question: how often a phrase that could go into a query appears in journal on-topic titles, in arXiv in-scope papers and in arXiv out-of-scope papers.
